## user

Tool result:
/*
 * Where the head is at any second of a cut. ROUND 7.
 *
 * The beat script (align/build_script.py -> <slug>.script.json) says what the
 * drill is doing. This file says what the HEAD does about it, as a list of
 * moves with absolute start times. Between moves the head holds a still.
 *
 *   read            facing the page
 *   tell / colour   turned away (the turn begins LEAD seconds before the
 *                   first word, because you look away before you speak)
 *   card            half turn: the monitor stays whole on the left, the card
 *                   stands in the room to the right
 *   review / press  facing
 *   recall          turned away for the whole recall, back for the 2nd press
 *
 * One turn is reused for every retell (R7-Q4), so the moves are pre-rendered
 * frames and this file only decides WHICH frame is showing.
 */
import {
  type Cam, type Move, MOVE_FRAMES, FPS, moveCam, CAM_FACING, CAM_TURNED, CAM_HALF, TURN_YAW,
} from "./headTurn";

export type Still = "facing" | "turned" | "half";
export type HeadPose =
  | { kind: "still"; name: Still; since: number }
  | { kind: "move"; move: Move; i: number; since: number };

/** Seconds the look-away leads the first spoken word. */
export const LEAD = 0.55;

type BeatLike = { kind: string; start: number; end: number; gi?: number };

type Ev = { t0: number; move: Move };

const ENDS: Record<Move, Still> = { away: "turned", back: "facing", halfAway: "half", halfBack: "facing" };
const dur = (m: Move) => MOVE_FRAMES[m] / FPS;

/** The move list for a beat script. Pure, so it can be unit-checked. */
export function headEvents(beats: BeatLike[]): Ev[] {
  const ev: Ev[] = [];
  const turnedKind = (k?: string) => k === "tell" || k === "colour" || k === "recall";
  for (let i = 0; i < beats.length; i++) {
    const b = beats[i];
    const prev = beats[i - 1];
    const next = beats[i + 1];
    if (b.kind === "card") {
      ev.push({ t0: b.start, move: "halfAway" });
      ev.push({ t0: Math.max(b.start + dur("halfAway"), b.end - dur("halfBack")), move: "halfBack" });
    } else if (turnedKind(b.kind)) {
      // Entering a turned run: look away before the first word when coming off
      // a read; on the word when a card has only just returned the head.
      if (!turnedKind(prev?.kind)) {
        const lead = prev?.kind === "read" ? LEAD : 0;
        ev.push({ t0: b.start - lead, move: "away" });
      }
      // Leaving it: land on the page exactly as the next beat starts.
      if (!turnedKind(next?.kind)) {
        const endAt = next ? next.start : b.end;
        ev.push({ t0: endAt - dur("back"), move: "back" });
      }
    }
  }
  ev.sort((a, b) => a.t0 - b.t0);
  return ev;
}

export function poseAt(ev: Ev[], t: number): HeadPose {
  let cur: Ev | null = null;
  for (const e of ev) { if (e.t0 <= t) cur = e; else break; }
  if (!cur) return { kind: "still", name: "facing", since: t };
  const into = t - cur.t0;
  const n = MOVE_FRAMES[cur.move];
  const i = Math.floor(into * FPS);
  if (i < n) return { kind: "move", move: cur.move, i, since: into };
  return { kind: "still", name: ENDS[cur.move], since: into - n / FPS };
}

export function poseCam(p: HeadPose): Cam {
  if (p.kind === "move") return moveCam(p.move, p.i / FPS);
  return p.name === "facing" ? CAM_FACING : p.name === "turned" ? CAM_TURNED : CAM_HALF;
}

/** Path under public/ of the pre-rendered plate for a pose. */
export function poseSrc(p: HeadPose): string {
  if (p.kind === "move") return `plates/r7/seq/${p.move}_${String(p.i).padStart(3, "0")}.jpg`;
  return `plates/r7/seq/${p.name}_000.jpg`;
}

/** 0..1, how far toward fully turned the head is. Drives the room effects. */
export function turnK(p: HeadPose): number {
  return Math.max(0, Math.min(1, poseCam(p).yaw / TURN_YAW));
}

/**
 * The living drift on a hold. The frames are stills; a head never is. Tiny,
 * slow, and applied to the whole room so overlays move with it.
 */
export function driftAt(p: HeadPose): { x: number; y: number; zoom: number } {
  if (p.kind === "move") return { x: 0, y: 0, zoom: 1 };
  const s = p.since;
  if (p.name === "facing") {
    return { x: -2.5 * Math.sin(s * 0.9), y: 2 * Math.sin(s * 0.6 + 1), zoom: 1 + 0.004 * Math.sin(s * 0.35) };
  }
  return { x: 4 * Math.sin(s * 0.7), y: 2.5 * Math.sin(s * 0.5 + 2), zoom: 1 + 0.003 * Math.sin(s * 0.3) };
}
import React from "react";
import { interpolate, spring, Easing } from "remotion";
import { SEC } from "../rrc/tokens";

/*
 * Things that hang in the room.
 *
 * Technique lifted from the HyperFrames kit's t2-lt-nameplate3d / t2-lb-bubble /
 * t2-lb-checkmark cards: a perspective stage, a card leaning in space with real
 * side depth, and a LOCAL bloom behind each card rather than one global glow.
 * The local bloom is what makes a card read as lit by the room instead of
 * pasted onto the frame.
 *
 * These deliberately do not use the app's palette. They must never be mistaken
 * for part of the drill, and here that is guaranteed by geometry: nothing in the
 * real drill floats in your room.
 */

const GLASS =
  "linear-gradient(135deg, rgba(255,255,255,.10) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,.035) 38%, " +
  "rgba(255,255,255,.014) 66%, rgba(255,255,255,.075) 100%)";

const Bloom: React.FC<{ w: number; h: number; rgb: string; a?: number }> = ({ w, h, rgb, a = 0.3 }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      position: "absolute", left: -w * 0.18, top: -h * 0.22,
      width: w * 1.36, height: h * 1.44, borderRadius: "50%",
      background: `radial-gradient(ellipse at 42% 78%, rgba(${rgb},${a}) 0%, rgba(${rgb},${a * 0.33}) 48%, transparent 74%)`,
      filter: "blur(38px)", pointerEvents: "none", zIndex: 0,
    }}
  />
);

/**
 * A card leaning in the room's space. Children sit on its face.
 *
 * ROUND 6: deepened, at his request for "more 3d effets and su h". The thickness
 * used to be one translucent slab 13px behind the face, which reads as a drop
 * shadow rather than as an edge. It is now a STACK of thin slices stepping back
 * in Z, each slightly darker, which is what gives an extruded object a visible
 * side you can see the light run down. Plus a bright top lip on the face, so
 * the near edge catches the room.
 *
 * Cost is a handful of divs per card and no filters, so it stays cheap to render.
 */
const SLICES = 7;

export const Plate3D: React.FC<{
  x: number; y: number; w: number;
  tiltX?: number; tiltY?: number;
  rgb?: string; enter: number;           // 0..1
  /** Extrusion in px. The face sits at Z 0 and the slices step back to -depth. */
  depth?: number;
  /* Cards that hang in the ROOM are absolutely placed in room space. The
     instruction cards are not objects in the room, they are the film stopping
     to talk, so they lay out in normal flow and let a flex parent centre them. */
  flow?: boolean;
  children: React.ReactNode;
}> = ({ x, y, w, tiltX = 7, tiltY = -9, rgb = "150,170,255", enter, depth = 15, flow = false, children }) => {
  const lift = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [26, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  const scale = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [0.9, 1], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  /* Cards arrive turned slightly further away and settle square, so the entry
     is a movement in space rather than a fade. */
  const swing = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [7, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });
  return (
    <div style={flow
      ? { position: "relative", perspective: 1200, perspectiveOrigin: "50% 50%", zIndex: 4 }
      : { position: "absolute", left: x, top: y, perspective: 820, perspectiveOrigin: "34% 58%", zIndex: 4 }}>
      <div
        style={{
          position: "relative", width: w, transformStyle: "preserve-3d",
          transform:
            `rotateX(${(tiltX + swing * 0.5).toFixed(2)}deg) ` +
            `rotateY(${(tiltY - swing).toFixed(2)}deg) ` +
            `translateY(${lift.toFixed(2)}px) scale(${scale.toFixed(3)})`,
          opacity: interpolate(enter, [0, 0.35], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: "clamp" }),
        }}
      >
        <Bloom w={w} h={130} rgb={rgb} />
        {/* Extruded side depth: thin slices stepping back in Z, darkening as
            they go, so the edge reads as a solid side rather than a shadow. */}
        {Array.from({ length: SLICES }, (_, i) => {
          const f = (i + 1) / SLICES;
          return (
            <div
              key={i}
              style={{
                position: "absolute", inset: 0, borderRadius: 16, zIndex: 1,
                transform: `translateZ(${(-depth * f).toFixed(2)}px)`,
                background: `rgba(${rgb},${(0.34 * (1 - f * 0.72)).toFixed(3)})`,
              }}
            />
          );
        })}
        <div
39:export const STEPS: Record<StepKey, Step> = {
40-  read: {
41-    n: 1,
42-    name: "Read it.",
43-    says: "Read about a sentence at a time, silently. Nothing is spoken yet.",
44-  },
45-  tell: {
46-    n: 2,
47-    name: "Tell it back.",
48-    says: "Look away and tell it back in your own words, out loud.",
49-  },
50-  colour: {
51-    n: 3,
52-    name: "Colour it.",
53-    says: "React to it out loud. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke.",
54-  },
55-  recall: {
56-    n: 4,
57-    name: "The Recall.",
58-    says: "The text goes. Say the whole argument back from memory.",

 * Note: When using the Node.JS APIs, the config file
 * doesn't apply. Instead, pass options directly to the APIs.

 * All configuration options: https://remotion.dev/docs/config

import { Config } from "@remotion/cli/config";
import { enableTailwind } from '@remotion/tailwind-v4';

/*
 * rspack is OFF deliberately. With `Config.setRspack(true)` on this machine the
 * bundler reports 'Bundling 100%' and exits 0 but emits no JavaScript at all:
 * build/ gets index.html and favicon.ico and nothing else. Every downstream
 * command then dies with a misleading "ENOENT ... bundle.js", which is
 * Remotion's source-map reader failing, not the real fault.
 *
 * Webpack (the default) bundles correctly. Verified 2026-09-09 by rendering
 * MyComp to a real 60-frame MP4. Do not re-enable without re-testing a render.
 */
// Config.setRspack(true);
Config.setVideoImageFormat("jpeg");
Config.setOverwriteOutput(true);
Config.overrideBundlerConfig(enableTailwind);

/*
 * The stimulus with a highlighter that hugs the words. ROUND 7.
 *
 * "The highlight should be more in touch." Round 6's light was a loose glow
 * with a wide sigma; it hovered over the line. This is a marker stroke: it
 * sits ON the words, tight, warm, slightly uneven at its ends like a real pen,
 * and it advances word by word at his measured reading pace. Nothing is
 * dimmed or hidden; words behind the stroke stay fully legible (R6 rule).
 */

export const Stimulus7: React.FC<{ chunks: Chunk[]; light: ReadLight | null }> = ({ chunks, light }) => {
  const litNs: number[] =
    light === null ? [] : light.ns === "all" ? chunks.map((c) => c.n) : light.ns;
  const runWords: { cn: number; wi: number }[] = [];
  for (const c of chunks) {
    if (!litNs.includes(c.n)) continue;
    c.text.trim().split(/\s+/).forEach((_, wi) => runWords.push({ cn: c.n, wi }));
  }
  const centre = light && runWords.length ? light.p * (runWords.length - 1) : -1;
  // Tight: the stroke covers ~2 words either side of the eye, and everything
  // already read keeps a faint trace so the path so far stays visible.
  const sigma = Math.max(0.9, (light?.spread ?? 1.7) * 0.6);

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\brainstorms\\2026-09-11-rrc-video-round8-written-in-air.md", "content": "# RRC Instructional Video, Round 8: text written in the air\nDate: 2026-09-11 · Goal: replace the dark slabs (intro card, speech bubbles, recall stack, placard) with text that is written in the air, lifelike, at the 4K effect budget; add blinks where they are natural.\nStatus: building\nContext: [[2026-09-10-rrc-instructional-video-round7]] (the shipped version, tagged `r7-shipped` in `projects/video`)\n\n## His brief (2026-09-11, voice, decoded)\n\nVerbatim: \"change the bezel to be more realistic, with a 4k sleek hyper use of maximum effects. Use the Hyperframe skill and blinking where it's natural. The exposure lift is good, and the blink is cool. Show me the best you can do. It should be very lifelike and real, rather than just a block. It should be almost written in the air, with some cool effects, but don't mess up. We've got a perfect version and might stick with it.\"\n\nAnswers to the carried open items:\n- 175 Kling credits: spent, accepted. Further generation allowed if needed. (None needed this round; everything here is DOM and CSS.)\n- Exposure lift: keep. Blink: keep, and add more where natural.\n- 40 deg turn: not mentioned; kept.\n- Audio seams / synthesised lines: still unanswered.\n\n## What \"the bezel\" means (my reading, stated so it can be corrected)\nThe frames at 3 s, 17 s and 128 s of the Q1 cut show dark rounded slabs with a lit rim: the Step 1 intro card looks like a tablet with a bezel, the bubbles and recall lines are black blocks. \"Rather than just a block ... almost written in the air\" points at those. The monitor's own bezel sliver at the left edge when turned is real plate pixels and is left alone.\n\n## Decisions (my call under the Round 7 Q4 licence)\n- **No boxes anywhere.** Every room card becomes light-written type: crisp warm-white face, dark diffuse ink halo for legibility over the window, a soft cast glow on the room behind it, and a faint local shade that has no edges. Nothing that reads as a panel.\n- **Written as spoken.** Bubble text appears word by word across the cue's real duration (the cue files carry phrase start/end; words are spread evenly inside a phrase). A small point of light travels each word as it forms: the pen. The previous phrase lifts and dissolves as the next writes in.\n- **Recall lines** are the same material, stacked; older lines recede (dimmer, slightly smaller, softer). Counter is small tracked caps with a hairline, not a badge.\n- **Intro cards** (Q1): \"STEP n\" in tracked caps with a hairline that draws in, the step name in Fraunces at display size written in the air, the cue line under it in Helvetica. Same anchor, same half-turn.\n- **Placard** on the desk: same type, no glass.\n- **\"Look Away\"** keeps its chrome face and gets the pen light and a once-only shimmer so the material matches.\n- **Effects, each tied to something real:** pen light (writing), focus pull on entry (blur 10px to 0, letters settle), shimmer sweep once after a line lands (light moving on a surface), motes drifting in the window light near the type (dust in a sunbeam, seeded PRNG, deterministic), a slow float on holds (the type hangs, it is not pinned).\n- **Blinks:** gaze-shift blink on the turn-away (existing) AND on the turn-back; periodic natural blinks on holds, first at ~3.5 s into a hold then every 4.5 to 7 s (seeded jitter), fast (2 frames down, 4 up), never inside the last 1.5 s before a move and never during the Space presses.\n- No Kling spend. No plate change. Head turn untouched.\n\n## Build log\n(appended as it happens)\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\Air.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { interpolate, Easing } from \"remotion\";\nimport { SEC } from \"../rrc/tokens\";\n\n/*\n * Type written in the air. ROUND 8.\n *\n * Round 7 put every room card on dark glass. He read the glass as a bezel, a\n * block hanging in the room. This is the replacement material: no panel at\n * all. The words themselves are the object.\n *\n * What makes a line of text read as WRITTEN IN THE AIR rather than pasted on\n * the frame, each tied to something an eye would actually see:\n *\n *   pen        a point of light travels each word as it forms, leaving a\n *              trail that fades. The writing hand.\n *   focus      a word arrives soft (blur 10 px) and resolves as it lands,\n *              the way an eye pulls focus onto something new.\n *   halo       a dark diffuse ink halo behind every letter. This is what\n *              keeps 48 px white type legible over a lit window with no box\n *              behind it. It has no edge, so it is never a panel.\n *   shade      a soft, edgeless dimming of the air behind the whole line,\n *              like the shadow a hanging object casts on haze.\n *   shimmer    once a line has landed, one band of light runs across the\n *              letters. Light moving on a surface. Happens once.\n *   motes      dust in the window light, drifting near the type. Seeded,\n *              so every render is the same.\n *   float      the line hangs; it is not pinned. A slow, small sway.\n *\n * Timing is in SECONDS since the line began (`since`), so a caller can drive\n * it from a cue's real start and the words land as he says them.\n */\n\nexport const AIR_FACE = \"#fff7e8\";\n/** Dark ink halo + a little warm bloom. Legibility first. */\nexport const AIR_HALO =\n  \"0 0 1px rgba(8,6,16,0.65), 0 1px 2px rgba(8,6,16,0.75), 0 3px 9px rgba(8,6,16,0.6), \" +\n  \"0 10px 30px rgba(8,6,16,0.5), 0 0 22px rgba(255,222,176,0.22)\";\n\n/* ----- deterministic randomness (HyperFrames rule: no Math.random) ----- */\nexport function mulberry32(seed: number) {\n  let a = seed >>> 0;\n  return () => {\n    a = (a + 0x6d2b79f5) >>> 0;\n    let t = a;\n    t = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), t | 1);\n    t ^= t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), t | 61);\n    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;\n  };\n}\n\n/** Progress 0..1 of word i of n, when the whole line writes over `write` s. */\nexport function wordProgress(since: number, i: number, n: number, write: number): number {\n  const slot = write / Math.max(1, n);\n  const each = Math.min(0.34, Math.max(0.16, slot * 1.7));\n  const start = i * slot;\n  return Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (since - start) / each));\n}\n\n/** Seconds the whole line takes to write when nothing else says. */\nexport function defaultWrite(n: number): number {\n  return Math.min(1.6, 0.22 + n * 0.075);\n}\n\nconst Pen: React.FC<{ e: number; size: number }> = ({ e, size }) => {\n  if (e <= 0 || e >= 1) return null;\n  const p = interpolate(e, [0, 1], [0.02, 1.0], { easing: Easing.inOut(Easing.quad) });\n  const glow = Math.sin(e * Math.PI);\n  const d = Math.max(6, size * 0.16);\n  return (\n    <>\n      {/* the trail: what the pen has already lit, fading behind it */}\n      <span\n        style={{\n          position: \"absolute\", left: 0, bottom: \"18%\", height: Math.max(2, size * 0.05),\n          width: `${(p * 100).toFixed(1)}%`, borderRadius: 4,\n          background: `linear-gradient(90deg, rgba(255,232,190,0) 0%, rgba(255,232,190,${(0.55 * glow).toFixed(2)}) 100%)`,\n          filter: \"blur(1px)\", pointerEvents: \"none\",\n        }}\n      />\n      {/* the point of light */}\n      <span\n        style={{\n          position: \"absolute\", left: `${(p * 100).toFixed(1)}%`, top: \"42%\", width: d, height: d,\n          marginLeft: -d / 2, marginTop: -d / 2, borderRadius: \"50%\",\n          background: \"radial-gradient(circle, #fffdf8 0%, rgba(255,240,210,0.9) 35%, rgba(255,220,170,0) 72%)\",\n          boxShadow: `0 0 ${d * 1.6}px ${d * 0.5}px rgba(255,228,180,${(0.75 * glow).toFixed(2)}), 0 0 ${d * 5}px ${d * 1.2}px rgba(255,210,150,${(0.35 * glow).toFixed(2)})`,\n          opacity: glow, pointerEvents: \"none\",\n        }}\n      />\n    </>\n  );\n};\n\nexport const AirWord: React.FC<{ text: string; e: number; size: number; pen?: boolean; last: boolean }> = ({\n  text, e, size, pen = true, last,\n}) => {\n  const y = interpolate(e, [0, 1], [size * 0.32, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  const blur = interpolate(e, [0, 1], [10, 0], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.quad) });\n  const sc = interpolate(e, [0, 1], [0.94, 1], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.back(1.2)) });\n  const op = interpolate(e, [0, 0.4], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" });\n  return (\n    <span\n      style={{\n        display: \"inline-block\", position: \"relative\", whiteSpace: \"pre\",\n        marginRight: last ? 0 : \"0.26em\",\n        transform: `translateY(${y.toFixed(1)}px) scale(${sc.toFixed(3)})`,\n        transformOrigin: \"0% 100%\",\n        opacity: op,\n        filter: blur > 0.05 ? `blur(${blur.toFixed(1)}px)` : undefined,\n      }}\n    >\n      {text}\n      {pen && <Pen e={e} size={size} />}\n    </span>\n  );\n};\n\n/** One band of light across the letters, once, after the line has landed. */\nconst Shimmer: React.FC<{ text: string; k: number; style: React.CSSProperties }> = ({ text, k, style }) => {\n  if (k <= 0 || k >= 1) return null;\n  const pos = interpolate(k, [0, 1], [-60, 160]);\n  const a = Math.sin(k * Math.PI);\n  return (\n    <span\n      aria-hidden\n      style={{\n        ...style, position: \"absolute\", left: 0, top: 0, right: 0, pointerEvents: \"none\",\n        color: \"transparent\", textShadow: \"none\",\n        backgroundImage: `linear-gradient(105deg, rgba(255,255,255,0) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,0) 38%, rgba(255,252,240,${(0.8 * a).toFixed(2)}) 50%, rgba(255,255,255,0) 62%, rgba(255,255,255,0) 100%)`,\n        backgroundSize: \"55% 100%\", backgroundRepeat: \"no-repeat\",\n        backgroundPosition: `${pos.toFixed(1)}% 0`,\n        WebkitBackgroundClip: \"text\", backgroundClip: \"text\",\n      }}\n    >\n      {text}\n    </span>\n  );\n};\n\n/** Dust in the window light. Deterministic. */\nexport const Motes: React.FC<{ seed: number; t: number; n?: number; on: number }> = ({ seed, t, n = 9, on }) => {\n  const rnd = mulberry32(seed);\n  const items = Array.from({ length: n }, () => ({\n    x: rnd(), y: rnd(), r: 2 + rnd() * 3.5, vx: 0.4 + rnd() * 0.8, vy: 0.6 + rnd() * 1.2, ph: rnd() * 6.28, tw: 0.4 + rnd() * 0.9,\n  }));\n  if (on <= 0.01) return null;\n  return (\n    <>\n      {items.map((m, i) => {\n        const x = (m.x * 100 + 4 * Math.sin(t * m.vx + m.ph)) ;\n        const y = ((m.y * 100 - t * m.vy * 3) % 120 + 120) % 120 - 10;\n        const tw = 0.35 + 0.65 * (0.5 + 0.5 * Math.sin(t * m.tw * 2.1 + m.ph));\n        return (\n          <span\n            key={i}\n            style={{\n              position: \"absolute\", left: `${x.toFixed(2)}%`, top: `${y.toFixed(2)}%`, width: m.r, height: m.r, borderRadius: \"50%\",\n              background: \"rgba(255,240,215,0.95)\", boxShadow: `0 0 ${m.r * 2}px ${m.r * 0.6}px rgba(255,228,190,0.45)`,\n              opacity: (tw * on * 0.85).toFixed(3), filter: \"blur(0.4px)\", pointerEvents: \"none\",\n            }}\n          />\n        );\n      })}\n    </>\n  );\n};\n\nexport type AirLineProps = {\n  text: string;\n  /** Seconds since this line began. Negative = not yet. */\n  since: number;\n  /** Seconds the words take to write. Default from word count. */\n  write?: number;\n  size: number;\n  weight?: number;\n  family?: string;\n  italic?: boolean;\n  letterSpacing?: number;\n  lineHeight?: number;\n  color?: string;\n  /** 0 = present, 1 = gone. Lifts and dissolves. */\n  leave?: number;\n  /** Overall presence (0..1) for callers that fade a whole group. */\n  opacity?: number;\n  pen?: boolean;\n  shade?: boolean;\n  motes?: boolean;\n  seed?: number;\n  style?: React.CSSProperties;\n};\n\n/**\n * A line (or wrapped paragraph) of type written in the air, word by word.\n * Lays out in normal flow, so a column of them stacks like text, not cards.\n */\nexport const AirLine: React.FC<AirLineProps> = ({\n  text, since, write, size, weight = 500, family = SEC.sans, italic = false, letterSpacing = -0.3, lineHeight = 1.28,\n  color = AIR_FACE, leave = 0, opacity = 1, pen = true, shade = true, motes = true, seed = 7, style,\n}) => {\n  const words = text.trim().split(/\\s+/).filter(Boolean);\n  const n = words.length;\n  const W = write ?? defaultWrite(n);\n  if (since < 0 || n === 0) return null;\n  const done = since - W;                       // seconds since the last word landed\n  const shimmerK = done > 0.15 ? (done - 0.15) / 0.9 : 0;\n  const allIn = wordProgress(since, n - 1, n, W) >= 1;\n  const sway = 2.2 * Math.sin(since * 0.85 + seed) ;\n  const lift = interpolate(leave, [0, 1], [0, -size * 0.9], { easing: Easing.in(Easing.quad) });\n  const lop = interpolate(leave, [0, 0.55], [1, 0], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" });\n  const lblur = interpolate(leave, [0, 1], [0, 9]);\n  const typo: React.CSSProperties = {\n    fontFamily: family, fontSize: size, fontWeight: weight, fontStyle: italic ? \"italic\" : \"normal\",\n    letterSpacing, lineHeight, whiteSpace: \"normal\",\n  };\n  const presence = Math.min(1, Math.max(0, opacity)) * lop;\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"relative\", display: \"block\",\n        transform: `translateY(${(sway + lift).toFixed(1)}px)`,\n        opacity: presence,\n        filter: lblur > 0.05 ? `blur(${lblur.toFixed(1)}px)` : undefined,\n        ...style,\n      }}\n    >\n      {shade && (\n        <div\n          aria-hidden\n          style={{\n            position: \"absolute\", left: \"-9%\", right: \"-9%\", top: \"-38%\", bottom: \"-38%\", zIndex: 0, pointerEvents: \"none\",\n            background: \"radial-gradient(ellipse at 50% 50%, rgba(10,8,18,0.42) 0%, rgba(10,8,18,0.26) 45%, rgba(10,8,18,0) 74%)\",\n            filter: \"blur(26px)\",\n            opacity: Math.min(1, since / 0.5),\n          }}\n        />\n      )}\n      {motes && (\n        <div aria-hidden style={{ position: \"absolute\", left: \"-6%\", right: \"-6%\", top: \"-30%\", bottom: \"-30%\", zIndex: 3, pointerEvents: \"none\" }}>\n          <Motes seed={seed * 31 + n} t={since} on={Math.min(1, since / 0.8)} />\n        </div>\n      )}\n      <div style={{ ...typo, position: \"relative\", zIndex: 1, color, textShadow: AIR_HALO }}>\n        {words.map((w, i) => (\n          <AirWord key={i} text={w} e={wordProgress(since, i, n, W)} size={size} pen={pen} last={i === n - 1} />\n        ))}\n        {allIn && <Shimmer text={words.join(\" \")} k={shimmerK} style={typo} />}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\n/** Small tracked caps with a hairline that draws in under it. ROUND 8: \"blinking where it's natural\".\n *\n * Round 7 blinked once, at the start of the turn-away (people blink when they\n * shift gaze). Two more places a real eye blinks:\n *\n *   on the turn-back     the same gaze shift, the other way.\n *   on a hold            every 4.5 to 7 s, jittered. A held stare is what\n *                        reads as a camera. The first comes 3.5 s into a hold\n *                        so it never lands on top of the settle.\n *\n * Never inside the last 1.5 s before a move, and never in a beat the caller\n * marks quiet (the Space presses, where a flicker would read as the screen).\n * Deterministic: the schedule is a pure function of the move list.\n */\n\n/** Seconds a natural blink lasts, and its shape: fast down, slower up. */\nconst BLINK_S = 0.22;\nfunction blinkShape(x: number): number {\n  if (x <= 0 || x >= 1) return 0;\n  return x < 0.33 ? x / 0.33 : 1 - (x - 0.33) / 0.67;\n}\n\nfunction hash(n: number): number {\n  let h = (n * 2654435761) >>> 0;\n  h ^= h >>> 13; h = Math.imul(h, 0x5bd1e995) >>> 0; h ^= h >>> 15;\n  return (h >>> 0) / 4294967296;\n}\n\n/** Absolute times of the natural blinks for a move list and cut length. */\nexport function blinkTimes(ev: Ev[], duration: number): number[] {\n  const out: number[] = [];\n  // Holds: from the end of each move to the start of the next.\n  const starts = ev.map((e) => e.t0);\n  const holds: [number, number][] = [];\n  let holdFrom = 0;\n  for (let i = 0; i < ev.length; i++) {\n    holds.push([holdFrom, starts[i]]);\n    holdFrom = starts[i] + dur(ev[i].move);\n  }\n  holds.push([holdFrom, duration]);\n  holds.forEach(([a, b], hi) => {\n    let t = a + 3.5 + hash(hi * 7 + 1) * 0.8;\n    let k = 0;\n    while (t < b - 1.5) {\n      out.push(t);\n      t += 4.5 + hash(hi * 7 + 11 + k) * 2.5;\n      k++;\n    }\n  });\n  return out.sort((x, y) => x - y);\n}\n\n/** 0..1 lid closure from the natural schedule at time t. */\nexport function naturalBlink(times: number[], t: number, quiet: boolean): number {\n  if (quiet) return 0;\n  for (const b of times) {\n    if (t >= b && t < b + BLINK_S) return blinkShape((t - b) / BLINK_S);\n    if (b > t) break;\n  }\n  return 0;\n}\nEOF\npython - <<'EOF'\np='src/pov/HeadRoom.tsx'\ns=open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()\ns=s.replace('''export const HeadRoom: React.FC<{\n  pose: HeadPose;\n  frameW: number; frameH: number;\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ pose, frameW, frameH, children }) => {''','''export const HeadRoom: React.FC<{\n  pose: HeadPose;\n  frameW: number; frameH: number;\n  /** ROUND 8: lid closure 0..1 from the natural blink schedule (headState.ts). */\n  blinkExtra?: number;\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ pose, frameW, frameH, blinkExtra = 0, children }) => {''')\ns=s.replace('''  // Blink: frames 0..5 of the full turn-away. The render is in progress (Q1, 4,200 frames at 1080p; the per-word blur filters make it heavier than Round 7). I'll pick up the motion check the moment it lands.

Where things stand:

**Done and committed** (`projects/video` `ba21b75`, with Round 7 safe under tag `r7-shipped`):
- Every dark slab is gone. Bubbles, intro cards, the step label and the recall column are now type written in the air: a point of light travels each word as it forms, letters arrive soft and pull into focus, a dark edgeless halo keeps them legible over the lit window, one band of light crosses each line after it lands, and seeded dust motes drift in the window light around it. Phrases write across the real duration of what you're saying, and the previous phrase lifts out as the next writes in.
- "Look Away" keeps its chrome face and gets the same pen light and sweep, so it's one material.
- Blinks: on the turn-away (kept), now also on the turn-back, plus a natural schedule on the holds (15 in Q1, none within 1.5 s of a move, none during a Space press). Exposure lift untouched. Head turn untouched.
- Zero Kling credits spent. Everything is DOM and CSS.

**One assumption to flag:** I read "the bezel" as the dark rounded slab behind the cards (the Step 1 card looked like a tablet with a bezel; the bubbles were "just a block"). The monitor's own bezel sliver at the frame edge when turned is real plate pixels and I left it. If you meant that sliver, say so and I'll treat it separately.

Next: check the 1080p cut at the turn moments and a phrase hand-off, then launch the three 4K renders and send you 720p copies.

## assistant

Five side-by-sides sent (left is what's live, right is the new cut). My honest read, having only Q1 at 1080p to compare so far:

**Where Round 8 is clearly better**
- The intro card (3 s) and step label (62 s). The slab in Round 7 sat across the monitor's edge and half over the screen; the new type stands in the gap beside the monitor, off the screen entirely. That was a real flaw in the live version, not just taste.
- The recall column (128 s). Same content, but it reads as words hanging in the room rather than a stack of black boxes, and the older lines recede so the newest is the clearest. This is the frame that best answers "written in the air".
- Material. Lit face, ink halo, motes, one shimmer, the pen light: it looks like one object family with "Look Away" now, where Round 7 had two languages (chrome sign, dark glass).

**Where Round 7 is better, and it matters**
- Reading the retell (17 s, 30 s). Round 7 shows the whole phrase the moment it starts, so a student can read ahead of your voice. Round 8 writes word by word at the pace you say it, which means at any instant the student has only the words already spoken. It looks alive; it is slower to read. For a film meant to teach the retell, being able to see the full thought at once has value.
- Legibility margin. The dark box guaranteed contrast. The new type is legible over the window, but with less headroom, and on a phone at 720p it will be closer to the line.

**Which one**
On craft, Round 8. On teaching function, the word-by-word writing is the one thing I'd change before it replaces the live version: write the phrase in about 0.6 s instead of across the whole cue, so it still arrives as writing but the full line is there almost immediately. That's a one-number change (`bubbleWrite`) and does not touch anything else.

I'd recommend: let the 4K renders finish as they are, you watch Q1 in full, and I make that timing change as the one revision. If you'd rather I stop the renders now and apply it first, say so; it costs about ten minutes of re-rendering.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

## Where it started
Picked up from the Round 7 handoff (theatre live on the RRC homepage with three dark-slab films). Joshua answered the carried items: 175 Kling credits accepted, further generation allowed, exposure lift and blink stay. He asked for "the bezel" to become realistic, 4K, maximum effects, "almost written in the air", using the HyperFrames skill, with Round 7 as the fallback. He then asked for a comparison, told me to decide, perfect it, and push it in, and to tell him when done.

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- "The bezel" read as the dark glass slabs (intro card, bubbles, recall stack, placard), not the monitor sliver. Stated as an assumption; he never contradicted it.
- No boxes anywhere. New material in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\video\src\pov\Air.tsx`: `AirLine`/`AirWord`/`AirLabel`/`Pen`/`Motes`. - `Cards.tsx`, `RecallRoom.tsx` rewritten on Air; `LookAway.tsx` gets pen + sweep + focus pull; `HeadRoom.tsx` blinks on turn-back too and takes `blinkExtra`; `headState.ts` has `blinkTimes`/`naturalBlink` (deterministic hold schedule, none within 1.5 s of a move, none in press beats); `POVInstructional.tsx` drives bubbles by `since`/`write`, previous phrase leaves over 0.5 s including across cue gaps.
